# Roadmap — xlite (Godot iso-tactics game)
**Maturity:** MVP+ (playable). **Stopping point:** a shippable desktop tactics game with enough content + polish; then mobile port, then maintain. Don't turn it into a live-service game.

## P1 — make combat feel great (the wedge: no-pay-to-wait, full-information)
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Telegraphed enemy intents (Into-the-Breach signature): planned target, threat overlay, intent arrows, damage preview {cat: game; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Wire pure combat predicates into battle.gd (suppression, overwatch, flanking, friendly-fire, destructible) — already-decomposed items exist {cat: game; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [x] [P1] [resolved 2026-09-10] Fog of war: 3-tier tile visibility (unexplored=black / remembered=dimmed / visible=full), driven by Manhattan sight range + real line-of-sight (IsoGrid.has_line_of_sight), persists across turn-rewind and mid-mission autosave. VERIFIED: tests/test_battle_fog_of_war.gd (276 lines) + full suite 2138/2138 green at commit time. Base system merged to develop (not yet promoted to main). {cat: game; size: L; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Fog-of-war follow-through (base is done, this is the remainder): (1) land the patrol-enemy AI extension — `_enemy_can_see_a_player`/`_patrol_step` in scripts/battle/battle.gd + `MissionData.enemy_patrol_routes`, tested via tests/test_battle_patrol.gd + tests/test_mission_data_patrol_route.gd — currently sitting on the local Mac `claude/feature` clone only, not yet on overnight/feature or develop; (2) split per-archetype enemy sight range instead of every enemy reusing the player's `SIGHT_RANGE` constant (battle.gd:20); (3) promote base fog-of-war from develop to main. (docs/LEVEL_DESIGN_GUIDELINES.md already exists and covers wall-pocket/patrol placement authoring — verified 2026-09-10, no longer open.) {cat: game; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [x] [P1] [resolved 2026-09-10] Left-click-drag camera panning (real playtest bug: `cam_pan` is middle-mouse-only, easy to miss/not have on a laptop trackpad). VERIFIED: tests/test_battle_left_drag_pan.gd 4/4 green (the initial 2 failures were a test-harness gap — GUT's synthetic InputEventMouseButton can't move the real OS cursor `_action_cell()` reads via get_global_mouse_position(), fixed by aiming the camera so a fixed viewport point maps to the target cell, not by changing production code). {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
## P2 — content + progression (~q2-3)
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Mission variety + campaign arc — Adopt Into the Breach's model: a small fixed set of ~6 objective types (Protect/Escort, Destroy Target, Hold Position N turns, Extract Before Timer, Non-Lethal/Don't-Kill using existing suppression predicates, Survive Escalating Waves) combined with varying hand-authored terrain/enemy layouts, since combinatorial reuse of few objective types is what lets a solo dev generate real variety without a mission generator. Structure the campaign as a short linear "chapter" spine (3 acts x ~5 missions, Wildermyth-style) with occasional binary branch points offering a choice of one of two hand-authored missions per branch, giving light replayability without doubling total authored content. Add win/lose variety beyond kill-all: partial success (objective met but with losses = degraded reward), hard mission-failure on timer/objective-death, and permanent unit loss carrying into the campaign to raise stakes. {cat: game; size: L; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: Into the Breach (intothebreach.fandom.com), Wildermyth campaign structure (wildermyth.com/wiki/Campaign)}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Roster depth: classes, abilities, synergy, upgrades — Ship 4-6 mechanically distinct classes tied directly to xlite's existing combat predicates rather than a generic RPG job system: Vanguard (melee/flanking specialist), Sentinel (overwatch/suppression specialist), Support (heal/buff/reposition), and Saboteur (destructibles/terrain control), each with 2-3 active abilities and one passive, following Fell Seal's lean "2 passives + abilities per class" scope rather than XCOM's deeper per-class trees. Design synergy explicitly as two-ability combos across classes (Mario+Rabbids pattern: one ability sets up, another cashes in) — e.g. Saboteur destroys cover to open a flank lane for Vanguard, or Sentinel's suppression guarantees Vanguard's flanking bonus on the same target. Upgrades are a flat per-class perk list (not subclassing/multiclassing) of ~3-4 tiers and ~10-12 total unlocks per class, gated by a mission-completion currency (not grindable XP) to keep authoring and balance small enough for one dev. {cat: game; size: L; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: Fell Seal: Arbiter's Mark classes (fellseal.fandom.com/wiki/Classes), Mario + Rabbids Kingdom Battle weapon synergy (news.ubisoft.com)}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Save/load hardening + settings + UX polish {cat: game; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Ability synergy/combo tags — pure two-ability combo detection for the roster-depth epic: new scripts/units/ability_synergy.gd (class_name AbilitySynergy) with a static is_combo(setup_tags: Array, cashin_tags: Array) -> bool and a small tag vocabulary (e.g. "sets_up_flank"/"cashes_in_flank", "sets_up_suppress"/"cashes_in_suppress"), plus tests/test_ability_synergy.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or ability_resolver.gd — pure detection logic only. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Per-class perk/upgrade tier data — new scripts/roster/class_perks.gd (class_name ClassPerks) defining a flat 3-4 tier perk list for each of the 5 EXISTING Unit.PlayerClass values (ASSAULT/HEAVY/SNIPER/MEDIC/SCOUT — not the roadmap's placeholder class names, which don't match the real enum) totaling ~10-12 unlocks/class, plus a validator static func following scripts/units/ability_stats_validator.gd's pattern (tier ordering, no duplicate unlock ids). Tests: tests/test_class_perks.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring purchase/spend into roster_manager.gd or battle.gd. {cat: game; size: M; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Partial-success degraded reward calc — new scripts/battle/partial_success_reward.gd (class_name PartialSuccessReward) with a pure static func computing a reduced credit payout when an objective is met but the squad took losses (e.g. payout(base_reward: int, units_lost: int, penalty_per_loss_pct: float) -> int), complementing scripts/battle/reward_calc.gd (RewardCalc.mission_payout has no loss-based degradation today — verified by reading the file, it's only 16 lines). Tests: tests/test_partial_success_reward.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd's _end_battle. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Objective failure predicate — new scripts/mission/objective_failure.gd (class_name ObjectiveFailure) adding the missing failure-side counterpart to scripts/mission/objective.gd's is_complete()/progress_ratio() (which only check success, verified by reading the file — no is_failed exists anywhere in the repo): a static is_failed(objective_type: int, ctx: Dictionary) -> bool covering timer-expiry (SURVIVE/DEFEND past max_turns without holding) and objective-critical-unit-death (ASSASSINATE-target-killed-as-a-player-unit / escort-target-died) cases per objective_type, mirroring objective.gd's match-on-objective_type style. Tests: tests/test_objective_failure.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd's end-of-turn checks. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Campaign branch-point resolver — new scripts/mission/campaign_branch.gd (class_name CampaignBranch) with pure logic resolving a binary campaign branch choice (per the campaign-arc design: occasional binary branch points offering one of two hand-authored missions) into the next mission id, e.g. resolve(chosen_option: int, branch_def: Dictionary) -> String, plus a validator confirming each branch_def has exactly 2 valid mission-id options. Tests: tests/test_campaign_branch.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into mission_hub.gd or mission_select.gd. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Extract misplaced Steam/UI helpers out of accuracy_curve.gd — scripts/battle/accuracy_curve.gd (a combat range-falloff pure class, verified via read: only falloff_percent/is_in_effective_band/best_range_for belong to its stated purpose) also contains `is_valid_steam_header(width,height)` and `validate_capsule_dimensions(width,height)` (Steam capsule-asset checks, unit-tested by tests/release/test_steam_asset_validator.gd which imports AccuracyCurve to test what is really Steam-release logic) and `get_long_press_threshold()` (an input-timing getter that is ALSO duplicated verbatim in scripts/units/unit.gd:236, independently tested by tests/test_unit_long_press.gd — confirmed via grep, both return ProjectSettings "xlite/input/long_press_threshold" default 0.5). Move the two Steam-capsule functions into a new scripts/release/steam_asset_validator.gd (class_name SteamAssetValidator, mirroring scripts/release/steam_readiness_check.gd's existing pattern) and update tests/release/test_steam_asset_validator.gd's preload to point at it; delete the duplicate get_long_press_threshold from accuracy_curve.gd and its test in tests/test_accuracy_curve.gd, keeping Unit.get_long_press_threshold() as the single source of truth. Does not touch battle.gd or mission_select.gd. {cat:test; size:S; multifile:yes; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] New difficulty_scaling.gd (Easy/Normal/Hard multiplier, pure calc) — verified via read that scripts/battle/enemy_scaling.gd only scales enemy stats by per-mission `tier` (stat_for_tier/health_for_tier/boss_stat), and a repo-wide grep for "difficulty" found only a mission-tier comment inside scripts/battle/battle.gd (banned file) — there is no player-selectable difficulty-level system anywhere (grid_renderer.gd/cover_object.gd/mission_select.gd only implement accessibility toggles like colorblind mode, not difficulty). Add new scripts/settings/difficulty_scaling.gd (class_name DifficultyScaling) with pure static funcs, e.g. enemy_stat_multiplier_percent(difficulty: int) -> int and player_damage_multiplier_percent(difficulty: int) -> int, for a small Easy/Normal/Hard enum (0/1/2), complementing (not calling into) EnemyScaling. Tests: tests/test_difficulty_scaling.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or mission_select.gd's settings panel. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Achievement registry: add milestones for systems that already exist but aren't tracked — scripts/steam/achievement_registry.gd's MAP (verified via read) has exactly 6 milestones (first_mission_complete, first_kill, first_soldier_lost, tech_tree_complete, roster_full, campaign_complete) and none reference the already-implemented StarRating (scripts/mission/star_rating.gd, stars() can return 3) or Extraction (scripts/mission/extraction.gd, count_extracted()) systems. Add 2 new data-only entries, e.g. "perfect_mission" and "full_squad_extraction", with unique non-empty Steam ids (e.g. ACH_PERFECT_MISSION, ACH_FULL_EXTRACTION so _validate_map's existing duplicate/empty checks still pass), plus new test cases in tests/test_achievement_registry.gd asserting steam_id_for() resolves them and validate() still passes. Pure data/logic change to an existing standalone file; no battle.gd/mission_select.gd involved. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] New itch_readiness_check.gd (fills a verified asymmetry with the Steam release checklist) — scripts/release/steam_readiness_check.gd has a real check_steam_dependencies() validator (checks the godotsteam addon exists), but scripts/release/itch_config.gd (verified via read) is just 3 bare constants (BUTLER_PATH, CHANNEL_NAME, GAME_ID) with zero validation logic — its test file tests/release/test_itch_config.gd only asserts the constants are non-empty/distinct strings, never checks they're actually valid for butler. Add new scripts/release/itch_readiness_check.gd (class_name ItchReadinessCheck) with a pure static func like is_valid_channel_name(name: String) -> bool enforcing itch/butler's channel-name convention (lowercase alphanumeric + hyphens only, matching ItchConfig.CHANNEL_NAME's existing value), following steam_readiness_check.gd's return-an-Array-of-problems pattern. Tests: tests/release/test_itch_readiness_check.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any build/export script. {cat:test; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] New player_stats_validator.gd (mirrors the existing EnemyStatsValidator/AbilityStatsValidator pattern) — scripts/units/enemy_stats_validator.gd (verified via read) is a pure bounds-check for an enemy stat dict (health, base_aim, attack_range, armor, attack_damage) used to sanity-check enemy blocks "before they're wired into Unit.configure_as_enemy()", and scripts/units/ability_stats_validator.gd does the same for ability defs — but there is no equivalent for the 5 real PlayerClass values (ASSAULT/HEAVY/SNIPER/MEDIC/SCOUT, confirmed via grep at scripts/units/unit.gd:17) whose base stats (health, max_health, base_aim, attack_range, move_range, armor — all confirmed field names via grep in unit.gd's configure_as_enemy/player-class blocks) are hand-authored inline with no bounds checking. Add scripts/units/player_stats_validator.gd (class_name PlayerStatsValidator) with a static ok(stats: Dictionary) -> bool following EnemyStatsValidator's exact fail-closed-on-missing-key style. Tests: tests/test_player_stats_validator.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into Unit's PlayerClass configuration code. {cat:test; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Mission validator: terrain theme + turn-limit checks — scripts/mission/mission_validator.gd (verified via read, 25 lines) has exactly 2 pure predicates today, `parallel_arrays_ok(m: MissionData) -> bool` and `target_index_ok(m: MissionData) -> bool`, both taking a MissionData and returning bool; MissionData.terrain_theme (scripts/mission/mission_data.gd, `@export var terrain_theme: String = "wasteland"`) is only ever consumed as "wasteland" or "city" (confirmed via grep: scripts/grid/grid_renderer.gd and scripts/grid/cover_object.gd both silently fall back to "wasteland" for any other value), and MissionData.max_turns (`@export var max_turns: int = 8`) has no lower-bound check anywhere despite TurnLimit.turns_remaining/is_survived assuming a positive value. Add two new static predicates to mission_validator.gd, matching the file's exact existing style: `static func terrain_theme_ok(m: MissionData) -> bool` (true iff m.terrain_theme == "wasteland" or m.terrain_theme == "city") and `static func max_turns_ok(m: MissionData) -> bool` (true iff m.max_turns > 0). Tests: new tests/test_mission_validator_terrain_theme.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring either into MissionData.is_valid(). {cat:test; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Injury recovery: progress percentage — scripts/roster/injury_recovery.gd (verified via read, 21 lines) has `missions_to_heal(wound_severity: int, per_mission: int) -> int` and `is_healed(rested: int, needed: int) -> bool` but no way to show partial healing progress (e.g. a progress bar). Add `static func recovery_progress_percent(rested: int, needed: int) -> int` to the same file: if needed <= 0 return 100 (nothing left to heal); otherwise return `clampi(rested * 100 / needed, 0, 100)`, mirroring scripts/battle/percent_util.gd's PercentUtil.clamp_pct pattern (verified via read). Tests: new tests/test_injury_recovery_percent.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI screen. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Input remapper: detect duplicate key bindings — scripts/settings/input_remapper.gd (verified via read, 77 lines) has DEFAULT_BINDINGS with exactly 7 actions (move_up, move_down, move_left, move_right, confirm, cancel, pause) and `current_bindings: Dictionary` mapping each action name (String) to its bound value (String), but nothing detects when two different actions get remapped to the SAME key, which would make both actions fire together. Add a new static pure function `static func find_conflicting_actions(bindings: Dictionary) -> Array[String]` to the same file: given a Dictionary shaped like current_bindings (action name String -> bound value String), return a sorted (alphabetical, ascending) Array[String] of every action key whose bound value is non-empty ("") and equal to another key's bound value; keys bound to "" (unbound) are never included even if several share "". Tests: new tests/test_input_remapper_conflicts.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into the settings UI. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Codex: tech entries missing their reveal key/wrapper — scripts/codex/codex_data.gd (verified via read, 132 lines) defines `enemy_key()`/`enemy_name()` and `soldier_key()`/`soldier_name()` for the bestiary/soldier codex categories, but has NO equivalent for tech (confirmed via grep -i tech on the file — zero matches beyond the file's own doc comment), even though scripts/codex/codex_manager.gd (verified via read, 105 lines) already defines `CAT_TECH: String = "tech"` and has `mark_enemy_seen(enemy_type: int) -> bool` / `mark_soldier_seen(player_class: int) -> bool` convenience wrappers but no mark_tech_seen. Add `static func tech_key(tech_name: String) -> String` to codex_data.gd: lower-case tech_name, then replace every space character with an underscore (e.g. "Improved Ammo" -> "improved_ammo", "Combat Stims" -> "combat_stims"). Add `func mark_tech_seen(tech_name: String) -> bool` to codex_manager.gd, implemented as exactly `return mark_seen(CAT_TECH, CodexData.tech_key(tech_name))`, mirroring mark_enemy_seen/mark_soldier_seen line-for-line. Tests: new tests/test_codex_data_tech.gd plus new test cases in tests/test_codex_manager.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring mark_tech_seen into TechManager.research_tech(). {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:yes; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Loot tier: minimum-level lookup (inverse of loot_tier) — scripts/battle/loot_tier.gd (verified via read, 14 lines) has `loot_tier(enemy_level: int) -> String` returning "common" (level <= 0 or < 3), "rare" (< 6), "epic" (< 9), or "legendary" (>= 9), but no way to go the other direction (e.g. showing "Legendary drops from level 9+ enemies" in a codex/tooltip). Add `static func min_level_for_tier(tier: String) -> int` to the same file: return 0 for "common", 3 for "rare", 6 for "epic", 9 for "legendary", and -1 for any other string. Tests: new tests/test_loot_tier_min_level.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Shield absorb: remaining shield after a hit — scripts/battle/shield_absorb.gd (verified via read, 11 lines) has `absorbed(shield: int, incoming: int) -> int` and `hp_damage(shield: int, incoming: int) -> int` (damage that overflows past the shield to HP), but nothing returns how much shield capacity is LEFT after absorbing a hit, which a shield-HP-bar UI would need. Add `static func remaining_shield(shield: int, incoming: int) -> int` to the same file: clamp shield to a minimum of 0 via `maxi(shield, 0)` exactly like absorbed() already does, then return `maxi(shield - incoming, 0)`. Tests: new tests/test_shield_absorb_remaining.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI/battle code. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Cooldown: turns-remaining counter — scripts/units/cooldown.gd (verified via read, 5 lines) has exactly one function, `ready(last_used_turn: int, cooldown: int, current_turn: int) -> bool`, with no way to show "N turns left" in a UI (distinct from the separate stateful scripts/units/ability.gd's Ability.turns_until_ready(), which tracks its own current_cooldown field rather than computing from turn numbers). Add `static func turns_remaining(last_used_turn: int, cooldown: int, current_turn: int) -> int` to the same file: return `maxi(cooldown - (current_turn - last_used_turn), 0)`. Tests: new tests/test_cooldown_remaining.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] TieBreaker highest-id tie-break helper — scripts/battle/tie_breaker.gd (verified via read, 18 lines) has `static func winner(a_stat: int, a_id: int, b_stat: int, b_id: int) -> int` and `static func lowest_id(ids: Array) -> int` (breaks ties toward the lowest id, returns -1 for an empty Array — confirmed via read/tests/test_tie_breaker.gd) but no counterpart for systems that want the opposite tie-break direction (e.g. "most recently spawned unit wins" using an incrementing id). Add `static func highest_id(ids: Array) -> int` to the same file: return -1 for an empty Array (mirroring lowest_id's -1-on-empty), otherwise return the maximum int value present in ids. Tests: new tests/test_tie_breaker_highest_id.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or initiative.gd. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] RageMeter UI progress percent — scripts/battle/rage_meter.gd (verified via read, 11 lines) has `static func gain(current: int, damage_taken: int, per_hp: int, cap: int) -> int` and `static func is_ready(current: int, threshold: int) -> bool` but nothing a rage/ultimate-charge meter bar could bind to for a 0-100 fill percentage (RageMeter is currently INERT/unwired — confirmed via grep and scripts/units/unit.gd:160-166's own doc comment on the `rage` field, so this is a pure addition with no behavior change to combat). Add `static func progress_percent(current: int, threshold: int) -> int` to the same file: if threshold <= 0 return 100 (nothing left to charge); otherwise return `clampi(current * 100 / threshold, 0, 100)`, mirroring scripts/battle/percent_util.gd's `PercentUtil.clamp_pct` clamp style (verified via read, 11 lines). Tests: new tests/test_rage_meter_progress_percent.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI scene or battle.gd. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] GrenadeArc remaining-reach indicator — scripts/battle/grenade_arc.gd (verified via read, 7 lines; consumed by scripts/battle/battle.gd's grenade-throw code via `GrenadeArc.max_throw_range` at battle.gd lines 1339, 1355, 2066, 3303, 3363 — confirmed via grep) has `static func max_throw_range(strength: int, base_range: int) -> int` and `static func in_throw_range(dist: int, max_range: int) -> bool` but nothing tells a throw-arc UI overlay how many tiles of unused reach remain at the cursor's current distance. Add `static func remaining_reach(dist: int, max_range: int) -> int` to the same file: return `maxi(max_range - dist, 0)` (0 once dist meets or exceeds max_range). Tests: new tests/test_grenade_arc_remaining_reach.gd. Deliberately stops short of touching battle.gd, which owns the actual throw-arc rendering and is hard-banned. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] ZoneOfControl overlap-cells (contested tiles) — scripts/grid/zone_of_control.gd (verified via read, 20 lines) has `static func controlled_cells(origin: Vector2i, size: Vector2i) -> Array` (the up-to-8 in-bounds neighbor cells of one unit) and `static func is_controlled(cell: Vector2i, origin: Vector2i) -> bool`, but nothing computes which cells TWO units both threaten at once — a "contested"/double-covered cell a squad-formation overlay would want to highlight differently. Add `static func overlap_cells(origin_a: Vector2i, origin_b: Vector2i, size: Vector2i) -> Array` to the same file: compute `controlled_cells(origin_a, size)` and `controlled_cells(origin_b, size)`, return only the Vector2i cells present in both, sorted ascending by x then by y (via `sort_custom`) for deterministic output across calls with the same inputs. Tests: new tests/test_zone_of_control_overlap.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or grid_renderer.gd. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
## P3 — ship + mobile (~year 2)
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Desktop release (Steam/itch) prep — Steam: create Steamworks account, pay the $100 Steam Direct fee (refundable after $1,000 gross revenue), then plan for a mandatory 30-day store-page-live period before release (realistic 4-6 week runway); build 5 required capsule assets (Header 920x430 is mandatory, plus Small/Main/Vertical/Library) plus 5+ screenshots at 1920x1080 and a 60-90s trailer leading with gameplay. Achievements/cloud-saves need GodotSteam, but note it was archived (read-only) July 2026 — verify it still builds against the current Godot 4.x export template before committing to it, or ship without Steam features first via itch.io (butler CLI upload, no fee, no wait) as the low-risk first release. {cat: infra; size: M; multifile: no; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: datahumble.com Steam Direct 2026 guide, GodotSteam GitHub (archived), itch.io butler docs}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Mobile port (Android first) — touch controls, safe-area — Remap desktop trackpad pinch-zoom/click-drag-pan to native two-finger pinch and one-finger drag on Camera2D (proven pattern: community PinchPanCamera / godot-touch-camera-2d assets), tap-to-select/tap-to-move for unit control since turn-based play has no real-time input-latency risk; use Godot's `DisplayServer.get_display_cutouts()` / `get_window_safe_area()` to keep the tactics HUD off notch/cutout regions rather than hand-rolled offsets. Biggest gotcha: Google Play now requires target API level 35 (Android 15) for all new submissions and AAB format, plus a mandatory Data Safety form (required even if xlite collects zero user data) — confirm the Godot Android export template + installed SDK/JDK17 toolchain are current before building, since template/API-level mismatches are the most common indie rejection cause. {cat: game; size: L; multifile: yes; research: none — completed 2026-09-10, sources: Android Developers target-sdk requirements, Godot Android export docs (developer.android.com/games/engines/godot)}
## Refuel 2026-09-17 — roadmap ran dry (zero [ready]/[needs-research] items left, everything below P1/P2/P3 was [decomposed]); 7 new verified, 27B-suited items added
Found by reading roadmap/xlite.md in full + the actual scripts/ and tests/ trees in repos/xlite (not invented). All 7 are pure-function/data additions to already-small, already-tested standalone files (or a verified dead-code dedupe) — no combat-AI reasoning (aggro/rage/retaliation excluded per the known 27B weak spot and its 3 tracked failing tests), no battle.gd/mission_select.gd (hard-banned), each self-verifiable via a new GUT test file or a grep.
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] UpkeepCost: actual-payable-amount helper (dedupes RosterManager's inline clamp) — scripts/roster/upkeep_cost.gd (verified via read, 14 lines) has `squad_upkeep(unit_count, per_unit) -> int` and `can_afford(funds, upkeep) -> bool`, but RosterManager.pay_squad_upkeep() (scripts/roster/roster_manager.gd, verified via read) reimplements "pay what's affordable, clamped at current funds" inline (`if not UpkeepCost.can_afford(_credits, cost): cost = _credits`) instead of calling a shared helper on the class that already owns this domain — the same kind of duplicate-logic gap the codebase already closed once for `get_long_press_threshold` (accuracy_curve.gd vs unit.gd, resolved 2026-09-14). Add `static func amount_payable(funds: int, upkeep: int) -> int` to upkeep_cost.gd: `mini(maxi(funds, 0), maxi(upkeep, 0))`. Tests: new tests/test_upkeep_cost_payable.gd. Deliberately stops short of rewiring RosterManager.pay_squad_upkeep to call it — that function is already live in the economy, leave the rewire for a follow-up pass. {cat:test; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] MultiObjective: progress ratio (multi-objective counterpart to Objective.progress_ratio) — scripts/mission/multi_objective.gd (verified via read, 20 lines) has `all_complete`/`any_failed`/`completed_count` over an Array of booleans, but no ratio, even though its sibling scripts/mission/objective.gd already has `progress_ratio(objective_type, ctx) -> float` for the single-objective case that a HUD progress bar binds to — MultiObjective exists specifically for the multi-objective missions this ratio is missing for. Add `static func progress_ratio(statuses: Array) -> float` to multi_objective.gd: `0.0` for an empty Array (consistent with `all_complete`'s empty-is-not-complete stance, not vacuously 1.0), else `float(completed_count(statuses)) / float(statuses.size())`. Tests: new tests/test_multi_objective_progress_ratio.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into mission_hub.gd or any objective-display UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] TileCost: remaining move-budget after a path — scripts/grid/tile_cost.gd (verified via read, 33 lines) has `cost_for_terrain`/`path_cost`/`affordable` for move-budget checks, but nothing reports how much budget would be LEFT after a given path, the same "remaining-after-spend" gap already closed for ShieldAbsorb.remaining_shield and GrenadeArc.remaining_reach (both landed 2026-09-14) — a movement-range overlay needs exactly this to show "N tiles of range left" past the cursor. Add `static func remaining_budget(terrain_types: Array, budget: int) -> int` to tile_cost.gd: if `path_cost(terrain_types)` is negative (impassable, mirroring path_cost's own sentinel) return -1; else `maxi(budget - path_cost(terrain_types), 0)`. Tests: new tests/test_tile_cost_remaining_budget.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into grid_renderer.gd's move-range overlay. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] TurnOrder: previous-actor lookup (reverse of next_actor_id) — scripts/turn/turn_order.gd (verified via read, 35 lines) has `order_by_initiative()` and `next_actor_id()` but no reverse-direction walk, and the whole file is currently INERT: confirmed via repo-wide grep, `TurnOrder` is referenced nowhere outside its own file, so this is a zero-risk pure addition with no behavior change (the same situation RageMeter was in before its `progress_percent` addition, also landed 2026-09-14). Add `static func previous_actor_id(order: Array, current_id: int) -> int` to the same file, mirroring `next_actor_id`'s structure but walking backward: -1 for an empty order, wrap to the LAST entry's id if current_id is order[0] or not found in order. Tests: new tests/test_turn_order_previous_actor.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any turn-manager or combat-log UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] ApPool: fill percentage for an AP-bar UI — scripts/units/ap_pool.gd (verified via read, 17 lines) has `can_act`/`spend`/`refill` for a unit's action-point pool, but — like RageMeter and InjuryRecovery before their own `progress_percent`/`recovery_progress_percent` additions (both 2026-09-14) — nothing gives a 0-100 fill percentage an AP-bar UI could bind to. Add `static func percent_full(current: int, cap: int) -> int` to ap_pool.gd: if `cap <= 0` return 0 (an uncapped/zero-capacity pool has nothing to be "full" of — deliberately the opposite convention from RageMeter/InjuryRecovery's "return 100 when nothing's left to do", since here a non-positive cap means there is no meaningful fill state at all); otherwise `clampi(current * 100 / cap, 0, 100)`. Tests: new tests/test_ap_pool_percent_full.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI scene or battle.gd. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Extraction: percent of squad extracted (feeds the existing "full_squad_extraction" achievement) — scripts/mission/extraction.gd (verified via read, 15 lines) has `in_zone()` and `count_extracted()` but no percentage helper, even though scripts/steam/achievement_registry.gd's "full_squad_extraction" milestone (added 2026-09-14, verified via read) needs exactly a ratio/percent check to ever fire for real — right now nothing in the repo computes it. Add `static func percent_extracted(unit_cells: Array, zone: Vector2i, radius: int, total_units: int) -> int` to extraction.gd: if `total_units <= 0` return 0; else `clampi(count_extracted(unit_cells, zone, radius) * 100 / total_units, 0, 100)`. Tests: new tests/test_extraction_percent.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into achievement_triggers.gd or any HUD. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Grid: dedupe two identical Manhattan-distance implementations — scripts/grid/manhattan.gd (`class_name Manhattan`, `manhattan_distance(a, b) -> int`) and scripts/grid/manhattan_distance.gd (`class_name ManhattanDistance`, `between(a, b) -> int` + `within(a, b, range) -> bool`) compute the EXACT same `absi(dx) + absi(dy)` formula under two different class names. Verified via repo-wide grep: neither class is referenced anywhere in scripts/ outside its own file — every real caller that mentions "Manhattan" (aoe_targeting.gd, vision.gd, damage.gd, confirmed via grep) does so only in a doc comment and computes its own distance inline, never calling either class. This is a second, independently-discovered instance of the exact duplicate-logic pattern already fixed once for `get_long_press_threshold` (accuracy_curve.gd vs unit.gd, resolved 2026-09-14). Keep manhattan_distance.gd (the superset — it already has `within()`); delete scripts/grid/manhattan.gd and tests/test_manhattan.gd. Re-run the grep for `class_name Manhattan` / bare `Manhattan.` (not `ManhattanDistance.`) immediately before deleting to reconfirm nothing new started referencing it. {cat:test; size:S; multifile:yes; research:repo}

## Refuel 2026-09-21 — roadmap ran dry again (Refuel 2026-09-17's 7 items fully decomposed/attempted; live doable count back to 0); 8 new verified, 27B-suited items added
Found by reading roadmap/xlite.md in full + the actual scripts/ and tests/ trees in repos/xlite on the GPU server (not invented). All 8 are pure-function additions to already-small, already-tested standalone files — no combat-AI/aggro/rage reasoning (excluded per the known 27B weak spot), no battle.gd/mission_select.gd (hard-banned per .queue-hard-banned-files), each self-verifiable via a new GUT test file or a grep.
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] BossPhase: phases-remaining counter — scripts/battle/boss_phase.gd (verified via read, 13 lines) has `phase_for_hp(hp_ratio, thresholds) -> int` and `is_enrage(hp_ratio, enrage_threshold) -> bool`; battle.gd only calls `BossPhase.is_enrage` (confirmed via grep, 2 real call-sites), and there is nothing a boss-fight HUD's phase-pip indicator could bind to for "N phases left". Add `static func phases_remaining(current_phase: int, total_phases: int) -> int` to the same file: `maxi(total_phases - current_phase, 0)`. Tests: tests/test_boss_phase_phases_remaining.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or any boss-HUD scene. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Overkill: wasted-damage percentage — scripts/battle/overkill.gd (verified via read, 13 lines) has `wasted(damage, target_hp) -> int` and `killed(damage, target_hp) -> bool`, but Overkill is referenced nowhere outside its own file (confirmed via repo-wide grep — INERT, same starting state RageMeter/TurnOrder/GridBounds were in before their own additions), and there's no percentage form for a combat-log "38% overkill" style callout. Add `static func wasted_percent(damage: int, target_hp: int) -> int` to the same file: return 0 if `damage <= 0`, else `clampi(wasted(damage, target_hp) * 100 / damage, 0, 100)`. Tests: tests/test_overkill_wasted_percent.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or any combat-log UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] HealCalc: heal-percent-of-missing-HP — scripts/battle/heal_calc.gd (verified via read, 16 lines) has `heal_amount`/`apply_heal`/`overheal_wasted` and is wired into battle.gd's Medic-revive path (`HealCalc.heal_amount(REVIVE_HP, medic.rank_level, REVIVE_HP_PER_RANK)`, confirmed via grep), but nothing expresses a heal as "% of missing HP restored" for a heal-preview tooltip. Add `static func heal_percent_of_missing(current_hp: int, max_hp: int, amount: int) -> int` to the same file: let `missing = max_hp - current_hp`; return 0 if `missing <= 0` (nothing missing to heal); else `clampi(amount * 100 / missing, 0, 100)`. Tests: tests/test_heal_calc_percent_of_missing.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or any heal-preview tooltip. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] PiercingLine: total damage across a full pierce — scripts/battle/piercing_line.gd (verified via read, 16 lines) has `targets_hit(line_length, max_pierce) -> int` and `damage_after_pierce(base, index, falloff_percent) -> int`, wired into battle.gd's line-pierce attack (2 real call-sites confirmed via grep), but nothing sums the falloff-decayed damage across every pierced target for a "this shot deals N total damage" preview line. Add `static func total_damage(base: int, target_count: int, falloff_percent: int) -> int` to the same file: sum `damage_after_pierce(base, i, falloff_percent)` for `i` in `range(maxi(target_count, 0))` and return the total, reusing `damage_after_pierce` rather than reimplementing its falloff math. Tests: tests/test_piercing_line_total_damage.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd's damage-preview UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Ammo: shots-remaining-before-reload counter — scripts/battle/ammo.gd (verified via read, 13 lines) has `shots_after_fire(current, shots) -> int` and `needs_reload(current, cost_per_shot) -> bool`; confirmed via repo-wide grep, Ammo is mentioned only in a doc comment in unit.gd and is never actually called anywhere (INERT, a zero-risk pure addition), and there is no "how many shots left before I must reload" counter an ammo-count UI needs. Add `static func shots_remaining(current: int, cost_per_shot: int) -> int` to the same file: if `cost_per_shot <= 0` return `maxi(current, 0)` (no cost means nothing to divide by); else `maxi(current, 0) / cost_per_shot`. Tests: tests/test_ammo_shots_remaining.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any ammo-count UI. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] RangeFalloff: remaining-range-to-max — scripts/battle/range_falloff.gd (verified via read, 16 lines) has `damage_at_range(base, dist, optimal, max_range) -> int` and `is_in_range(dist, max_range) -> bool`; confirmed via repo-wide grep, RangeFalloff is INERT (no caller outside its own file/tests — distinct from the already-wired GrenadeArc, a different throw-arc domain), and lacks the same "remaining reach" helper already added for GrenadeArc.remaining_reach and TileCost.remaining_budget (both landed 2026-09-14/19) — a ranged-attack indicator overlay needs this for its own domain. Add `static func remaining_range(dist: int, max_range: int) -> int` to the same file: `maxi(max_range - dist, 0)`. Tests: tests/test_range_falloff_remaining_range.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd or any range-indicator overlay. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] ChainDamage: total damage across a full chain — scripts/battle/chain_damage.gd (verified via read, 15 lines) has exactly one function, `at_jump(base_damage, jump_index, decay_percent) -> int`, wired into battle.gd's single-jump chain-arc attack (`ChainDamage.at_jump(primary_damage, 1, CHAIN_DECAY_PERCENT)`, confirmed via grep, currently only ever called with a fixed jump_index of 1), but nothing sums the decayed damage across a longer N-jump chain for a "total chain damage" preview, the same aggregate gap just identified for PiercingLine.total_damage but in this file's own separate chain-lightning domain. Add `static func total_over_chain(base_damage: int, jumps: int, decay_percent: int) -> int` to the same file: sum `at_jump(base_damage, i, decay_percent)` for `i` in `range(maxi(jumps, 0))` and return the total. Tests: tests/test_chain_damage_total_over_chain.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd, which currently only performs a single fixed jump. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] GridBounds: distance-to-nearest-edge — scripts/grid/grid_bounds.gd (verified via read, 12 lines) has `in_bounds(cell, size) -> bool` and `clamp_cell(cell, size) -> Vector2i`; confirmed via repo-wide grep, GridBounds is INERT (no caller outside its own file/tests), and has no pure geometry helper for "how many tiles from cell to the nearest map edge", useful for e.g. an extraction-zone-near-edge overlay or a spawn-safety-margin check. Add `static func distance_to_edge(cell: Vector2i, size: Vector2i) -> int` to the same file: `mini(mini(cell.x, size.x - 1 - cell.x), mini(cell.y, size.y - 1 - cell.y))`. Tests: tests/test_grid_bounds_distance_to_edge.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into grid_renderer.gd or any overlay. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
## Refuel 2026-09-22 (fixed a format bug + refill) — the 8 items added 2026-09-21 (BossPhase/Overkill/HealCalc/PiercingLine/Ammo/RangeFalloff/ChainDamage/GridBounds) were all ALREADY IMPLEMENTED in scripts/ + tests/ (confirmed via read — every function and its test file already exists in the live repo), so their backlog headers were deleted rather than completed. 6 new verified items added below to replace them (1 dead-code dedupe + 5 pure-function additions to already-small, already-tested standalone files), each decomposed to 27B-sized granular steps. No combat-AI/aggro/rage reasoning, no battle.gd/mission_select.gd (hard-banned per .queue-hard-banned-files).
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Dedupe scripts/battle/upkeep.gd — a second, orphaned `class_name Upkeep` with its own `amount_payable(funds, upkeep)` clamp (float variant) that duplicates `scripts/roster/upkeep_cost.gd`'s `UpkeepCost.amount_payable` (int variant, already tested via tests/test_upkeep_cost_payable.gd, already the canonical helper per the 2026-09-14 roadmap item). Verified via repo-wide grep: `Upkeep.` has zero callers anywhere outside its own file and it has no test file — a leftover from a mis-decomposition during the 2026-09-21 recovery flow (visible in OVERNIGHT_PROGRESS.md's `recovered from a parked item` trail for tests/test_upkeep_cost_payable.gd). Delete scripts/battle/upkeep.gd. {cat:test; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] XpCurve: level-for-xp inverse lookup — scripts/units/xp_curve.gd (verified via read, 7 lines) has `xp_for_level(level) -> int` but no reverse lookup, even though a level-up progress UI needs "what level is N xp worth" as much as "how much xp does level N need". Add `static func level_for_xp(xp: int) -> int` to the same file: 1 if xp<=0, else walk level upward from 1 while `xp_for_level(level+1) <= xp`. Tests: new tests/test_xp_curve_level_for_xp.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] CritOdds: bonus needed for a guaranteed crit — scripts/battle/crit_odds.gd (verified via read, 8 lines) has `crit_percent(base_crit, flank_bonus, height_bonus) -> int` and `crit_damage` but nothing tells a "crit build" tooltip how many more crit-percent points are needed to reach a guaranteed 100%. Add `static func bonus_needed_for_guaranteed(base_crit: int, flank_bonus: int, height_bonus: int) -> int` to the same file: `maxi(0, 100 - (base_crit + flank_bonus + height_bonus))`. Tests: new tests/test_crit_odds_bonus_needed.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] BurstFire: hit-percent needed to guarantee a kill — scripts/battle/burst_fire.gd (verified via read, 7 lines) has `shots_from_ammo` and `expected_damage(shots, per_shot, hit_percent)` but no inverse for a "will this burst kill?" preview. Add `static func hit_percent_needed(target_hp: int, shots: int, per_shot: int) -> int` to the same file: 100 if shots<=0 or per_shot<=0; else `clampi(ceili(float(target_hp) * 100.0 / float(shots * per_shot)), 0, 100)`. Tests: new tests/test_burst_fire_hit_percent_needed.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] ArmorPen: damage-reduced percentage — scripts/battle/armor_pen.gd (verified via read, 8 lines) has `effective_armor` and `mitigated(raw, armor, pen) -> int` but no percent form for a damage-preview tooltip ("30% reduced by armor"). Add `static func damage_reduced_percent(raw: int, armor: int, pen: int) -> int` to the same file: 0 if raw<=0; else `clampi((raw - mitigated(raw, armor, pen)) * 100 / raw, 0, 100)`, reusing `mitigated` rather than recomputing its math. Tests: new tests/test_armor_pen_reduced_percent.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] SlotName: index-from-label inverse — scripts/save/slot_name.gd (verified via read, 5 lines) has `slot_label(index) -> String` ("Slot %d", 1-based) but no reverse parse for a save-slot picker that stores/receives the label text. Add `static func index_from_label(label: String) -> int` to the same file: -1 if it doesn't start with "Slot ", -1 if the suffix isn't a valid int, else `maxi(suffix.to_int() - 1, 0)`, matching the codebase's existing -1-not-found sentinel convention (e.g. TieBreaker.lowest_id). Tests: new tests/test_slot_name_index_from_label.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}

## Refuel 2026-09-22 (deep pass) — exhaustive read of every scripts/ subtree not already covered by the 09-17/09-21/09-22 refuels; 27 new verified items (24 pure-function/data additions to already-small, already-tested standalone files + 3 verified dead-code/duplicate-class deletions), plus 1 flagged-but-not-actioned finding. All verified by reading the actual current file content and cross-checking every claim with a repo-wide grep (not invented, not inferred from names) — no combat-AI/aggro/rage reasoning, no battle.gd/mission_select.gd (hard-banned per .queue-hard-banned-files). Covered subsystems this pass: scripts/battle (cover/dodge/salvage/execute/height/reload/threat/hazard/counter/falloff/lifesteal/mark/streak/variance/degradation/catapult/flank_bonus), scripts/grid (diagonal/spiral/bresenham/los/distance), scripts/roster (stat_growth), scripts/units (xp_curve/xp_calc dupe), scripts/mission (star_rating), scripts/turn (round_label), scripts/ui (touch_target), scripts/audio (audio_state), scripts/platform.
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] CoverValue: cover-tier-needed-for-target-defense (inverse of defense_pct) — scripts/battle/cover_value.gd (verified via read, 9 lines) has `defense_pct(cover_tier, per_tier, cap) -> int` and `reduced_hit(base_hit, cover_tier, per_tier, cap) -> int` but no reverse lookup for a "what cover tier gives me at least X% defense" tooltip. Add `static func cover_tier_for_defense_pct(target_pct: int, per_tier: int, cap: int) -> int`: -1 if `per_tier <= 0`; 0 if `target_pct <= 0`; -1 if `target_pct > cap` (unreachable regardless of tier, since defense_pct caps at `cap`); else `ceili(float(target_pct) / float(per_tier))`. Tests: new tests/test_cover_value_tier_for_defense.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI tooltip. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] DodgeChance: agility-needed-for-target-dodge (inverse of dodge_percent) — scripts/battle/dodge_chance.gd (verified via read, 9 lines) has `dodge_percent(agility, incoming_accuracy) -> int` (clamped 0-95, `incoming_accuracy` param currently unused in the formula — verified, not this item's concern) and `effective_hit`, but no reverse lookup for a "how much agility do I need for 50% dodge" build-planning tooltip. Add `static func agility_needed_for_dodge(target_dodge_percent: int) -> int`: 0 if `target_dodge_percent <= 0`; -1 if `target_dodge_percent > 95` (95 is dodge_percent's hard cap, unreachable beyond it); else `ceili(float(target_dodge_percent) / 2.0)`. Tests: new tests/test_dodge_chance_agility_needed.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] SalvageValue: total scrap across a tiered kill batch (composes existing helpers) — scripts/battle/salvage_value.gd (verified via read, 9 lines) has `scrap_for_tier(base, tier, per_tier) -> int` (scrap for ONE kill at a tier) and `total_scrap(count, per_unit) -> int` (total for N kills at a FLAT per-unit value), but nothing combines them for "N kills, all at the same tier" without the caller doing `total_scrap(count, scrap_for_tier(...))` by hand. Add `static func total_scrap_for_tier(count: int, base: int, tier: int, per_tier: int) -> int` implemented as exactly `return total_scrap(count, scrap_for_tier(base, tier, per_tier))`, reusing both existing helpers rather than reimplementing their math. Tests: new tests/test_salvage_value_total_for_tier.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any post-mission summary UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] ExecuteThreshold: percent above the execute line (HP-bar UI helper) — scripts/battle/execute_threshold.gd (verified via read, 9 lines) has `can_execute(hp, max_hp, threshold_percent) -> bool` (a boolean gate) but nothing tells a "how close to executable" HP-bar overlay how many percentage points above the threshold the target currently sits. Add `static func percent_above_threshold(hp: int, max_hp: int, threshold_percent: int) -> int`: 0 if `max_hp <= 0`; else let `hp_percent = clampi(hp * 100 / max_hp, 0, 100)`, return `maxi(hp_percent - threshold_percent, 0)`. Tests: new tests/test_execute_threshold_percent_above.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any HP-bar overlay. {cat:ui; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] HeightDamage: height-levels-needed-for-a-target-bonus (inverse of bonus_percent) — scripts/battle/height_damage.gd (verified via read, 10 lines) has `bonus_percent(attacker_z, target_z, per_level) -> int` and `total(base, bonus_percent) -> int` but no reverse lookup for "how much higher do I need to be for +30% damage" positioning-hint. Add `static func levels_needed_for_bonus(target_percent: int, per_level: int) -> int`: -1 if `per_level <= 0`; 0 if `target_percent <= 0`; else `ceili(float(target_percent) / float(per_level))`. Tests: new tests/test_height_damage_levels_needed.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any positioning overlay. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] StatGrowth: levels-needed-to-reach-a-target-stat (inverse of stat_at_level) — scripts/roster/stat_growth.gd (verified via read, 10 lines) has `stat_at_level(base, level, per_level) -> int` and `total_from_levels` but no reverse lookup for a "what level gets me to X health" roster-planning tooltip. Add `static func levels_needed_for_stat(base: int, target_stat: int, per_level: int) -> int`: 1 if `target_stat <= base` (already there at the base level); -1 if `per_level <= 0` (can never grow further); else `1 + ceili(float(target_stat - base) / float(per_level))`. Tests: new tests/test_stat_growth_levels_needed.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into any roster UI. {cat:gameplay; size:S; multifile:no; research:repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] ReloadCost: AP remaining after paying to reload (reuses ap_to_reload) — scripts/battle/reload_cost.gd (verified via read, 11 lines) has `ap_to_reload(missing, per_clip) -> int` and `can_afford(ap, cost) -> bool` but nothing reports the AP left over after paying, the same "remaining-after-spend" gap already closed for ShieldAbsorb/GrenadeArc/TileCost. Add `static func ap_after_reload(ap: int, missing: int, per_clip: int) -> int` implemented as `return maxi(ap - ap_to_reload(missing, per_clip), 0)`, reusing `ap_to_reload` rather than recomputing it. **Flagged but NOT decomposed (needs a human/Claude judgment call, too ambiguous for the fleet)**: scripts/battle/damage_preview.gd (verified via read, 15 lines) has TWO unrelated-looking functions — `predicted_damage(base, cover, flanked)` (fully covered by tests/test_damage_preview.gd, 6 cases) and `calculate_projected_damage(attacker_stats: Dictionary, target_stats: Dictionary, intent_data: Dictionary)`, which has ZERO test coverage and an `intent_data` parameter that is accepted but never read anywhere in the function body (verified via read — the function only touches `attacker_stats`/`target_stats`). This could be an incomplete feature (intent-based damage preview never finished) or genuinely dead code; either way, deciding which requires understanding intended design, not just a mechanical grep, so it's left for a Claude-owned look rather than a fleet decomposition.

## Stopping point
Feature-complete desktop game + mobile port = DONE. Then balance patches + bug-fixes only.

## Refuel 2026-09-25 (Claude research pass — roadmap was fully [decomposed]/dry)
Read roadmap.md in full (all prior Refuel passes 2026-09-10 through 09-22), PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md, README.md, the last 40 commits, and the current scripts/+tests/ file listing (not invented). Recent history is almost entirely combat-mechanics micro-helpers (percent/remaining/inverse accessors on already-tiny battle/grid files) across three deep passes — that vein is heavily mined, so this pass deliberately balances toward the systems PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md itself flags as thin: theme/content identity, economy, mission variety, and post-mission UI. It also promotes the one long-stale, never-actioned design note found in PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md's 2026-08-28 "Theme" section. No combat-AI/aggro/rage reasoning, no battle.gd/mission_select.gd edits (hard-banned per `.queue-hard-banned-files`).

PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md's 2026-08-28 "Theme: post-apocalyptic" section locked a direction (nuclear war + a rogue military AI; MACHINES = armored/ranged/precise re-skinning Sentry/Enforcer/Recon-Drone/Chassis, MUTANTS = fast/organic/melee-swarm re-skinning Stalker/Bloater/Howler/Brute) and said "QUEUE can do once confirmed: a `faction` enum... re-skin display names... new themed enemy archetypes as data" — but a month later, `file_tree.txt` shows no `faction.gd` and no faction field anywhere under `scripts/units/` or `scripts/battle/`, and none of the last 40 commits touch it. The "CONFIRM before the data re-skin" gate it was waiting on is already satisfied — memory's locked art-direction canon describes the identical post-apocalyptic-wasteland-plus-advanced-tech theme — so this is promoted to a real, ready item rather than left flagged.
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Enemy faction enum + themed archetype re-skin (MACHINE/MUTANT) — the design direction from PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md's Theme section (above) has sat unactioned for a month despite its own confirmation gate already being satisfied by memory's locked art-direction canon; `Unit.EnemyType` currently has exactly 10 archetypes (confirmed via recent_done.txt's cross-archetype invariant test iterating `Unit.EnemyType.values()`), none of them faction-tagged. Add a new `scripts/units/unit_faction.gd` (class_name UnitFaction) with a `Faction` enum (MACHINE=0, MUTANT=1) and `static func faction_for_enemy_type(enemy_type: int) -> int` mapping each existing EnemyType value to MACHINE or MUTANT per the theme doc's Sentry/Enforcer/Recon-Drone/Chassis-vs-Stalker/Bloater/Howler/Brute split, plus a parallel display-name table re-skinning the current Grunt/Heavy/Sniper/Drone labels into the themed names. Tests: new tests/test_unit_faction.gd asserting every EnemyType value resolves to exactly one faction and the two factions partition the full 10-value set with no gaps/overlaps. Deliberately stops short of touching battle.gd, unit.gd's stat blocks, or any art/animation — pure enum + name-table addition only. {cat: gameplay; size: M; multifile: no; research: none — direction already locked 2026-08-28, sources: PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md Theme section + memory reference_xlite-art-direction.md}

Verified via file_tree.txt that there is no equipment/gear/item file anywhere under scripts/roster/ or scripts/units/, and RosterManager's only credit sinks today are `recruit()` and `pay_squad_upkeep()` plus TechManager.research_tech — soldiers have zero purchasable, per-unit customization despite the combat layer already exposing plenty of modifier axes (ArmorPen.mitigated, CritOdds.crit_percent, CoverValue.defense_pct) a gear slot could plug bonuses into later. This is a genuine, currently-absent economy + progression system.
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Equipment/Loadout stat-modifier system (new economy sink + progression axis) — add new scripts/roster/equipment.gd (class_name Equipment) with a small fixed item table (3-4 tiers across a few slots, e.g. WEAPON_MOD/ARMOR/UTILITY) and pure static funcs: `cost_for_tier(tier: int) -> int` and `stat_bonus_for_tier(tier: int, per_tier: int) -> int` (mirrors StatGrowth.stat_at_level's additive-per-tier shape, scripts/roster/stat_growth.gd), plus a validator `static func is_valid_slot(slot_name: String) -> bool` following EnemyStatsValidator's fail-closed style. Tests: tests/test_equipment.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into RosterManager.create_unit_from_entry or any shop UI — pure data/calc layer first. {cat: gameplay; size: L; multifile: no; research: none — new system, sources: XCOM 2 gear-slot economy, Fell Seal equipment slots}

The already-decomposed Mission Variety epic explicitly bets on "combinatorial reuse of few objective types... is what lets a solo dev generate real variety without a mission generator," but nothing yet varies a mission run-to-run beyond its fixed authored data — mission_validator.gd's documented scope confirms MissionData has no modifier/directive field at all today.
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Mission Directives: per-mission modifier rolls (cheap combinatorial variety, no new authored content) — add new scripts/mission/mission_directive.gd (class_name MissionDirective) with a small fixed directive table (e.g. NONE/NIGHT_OPS/REINFORCEMENTS/LOW_AMMO, 4-5 entries) and pure static funcs: `aim_modifier_percent(directive: int) -> int` (e.g. NIGHT_OPS = -10, others = 0) and `bonus_enemy_count(directive: int) -> int` (REINFORCEMENTS = 1, others = 0), plus `static func is_valid_directive(directive: int) -> bool`. Tests: tests/test_mission_directive.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into MissionData, battle.gd, or mission_select.gd's briefing screen — pure data/calc layer only. {cat: gameplay; size: M; multifile: no; research: none — new system, sources: FTL sector-event modifiers, XCOM 2 Dark Events}

RewardCalc.mission_payout(...), StarRating.stars(...), and Extraction.percent_extracted(...) each compute one facet of a post-mission report, but nothing combines them into one result a summary screen could bind to in a single call, the same "compose, don't duplicate" pattern SalvageValue.total_scrap_for_tier already used successfully.
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] MissionSummary: post-mission totals aggregator (composes 3 already-verified, currently-unwired helpers) — add new scripts/mission/mission_summary.gd (class_name MissionSummary) with `static func build(won: bool, kills: int, captures: int, mission_reward: int, kill_reward: int, capture_reward: int, turns_used: int, par_turns: int, casualties: int, unit_cells: Array, zone: Vector2i, radius: int, total_units: int) -> Dictionary` returning `{"credits": RewardCalc.mission_payout(...), "stars": StarRating.stars(...), "extraction_percent": Extraction.percent_extracted(...)}`, reusing all three helpers rather than recomputing their math. Tests: tests/test_mission_summary.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into battle.gd's `_end_battle` or any summary-screen scene. {cat: gameplay; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}

scripts/roster/injury_recovery.gd only tracks TIME-TO-HEAL with no permanent consequence once healed, even though the Mission Variety epic's own stated design goal is "permanent unit loss carrying into the campaign to raise stakes" — today a soldier who takes a severe wound and survives comes back with zero lasting cost, undercutting that stakes goal for anyone who doesn't outright lose the unit.
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Veteran Scars: permanent post-injury stat trade-off (fills the gap between "heals fully" and "dies forever") — add new scripts/roster/scar.gd (class_name Scar) with pure static funcs: `static func should_scar(wound_severity: int, severe_threshold: int) -> bool` (true only above the threshold) and `static func stat_penalty_percent(wound_severity: int, per_severity: int, cap: int) -> int` (clamped, mirroring CoverValue/CritOdds's existing clamp-and-cap style), representing a permanent small stat malus applied once per wound. Tests: tests/test_scar.gd. Deliberately stops short of wiring into RosterManager or injury_recovery.gd's healing flow — pure calc layer only. {cat: gameplay; size: M; multifile: no; research: none — new system, sources: Darkest Dungeon negative quirks, XCOM 2 wound-scar trade-offs}

Tests: tests/test_loadout_preset.gd. ## Research refuel 2026-09-30 (interactive Claude research pass; 11 item(s))
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] LIVE BUG: fallen soldiers are never dropped from the roster and casualties are never detected — scripts/battle/battle.gd `_remove_unit` (~4322) does `_units.erase(u)` for a killed/bled-out PLAYER unit, but `_end_battle` (~4419) checks `_units` for `health<=0 and not is_downed` to set `any_soldier_lost` (always false: StarRating casualties=0, `flawless` always true, `on_mission_losses` achievement wrong) and `RosterManager.sync_from_battle(_units)` (roster_manager.gd:426) re-adds every name absent from battle_units via its carry-over loop (469-471), so permadeath silently never happens. Fix: track `_fallen_names` (Array[String]) in `_remove_unit` for team PLAYER (also snapshot/restore it in `_capture_turn_snapshot`/`_rewind_turn` and the autosave/resume dict), set any_soldier_lost from it, pass it to a new optional `sync_from_battle(units, fallen_names=[])` param that skips those names in the carry-over. Add tests in tests/test_roster_manager.gd + a battle test that kills one soldier via `_kill_or_down` with a big overkill then calls `_end_battle(true)`. {cat: game; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] LIVE BUG: mid-battle autosave/resume corrupts enemy patrol routes — scripts/battle/battle.gd `_unit_state_to_json` (~2859) converts only `cell` and `modulate`, but `Unit.capture_state()` (unit.gd:966-980) also emits `patrol_route` (Array of Vector2i) and `patrol_index`; after a JSON round-trip the waypoints come back as strings (and index as float), so `_patrol_step` (~3457) `var target: Vector2i = e.patrol_route[e.patrol_index]` errors on the first enemy turn after resuming a mission with patrol enemies. tests/test_battle_persistence.gd has zero patrol coverage. Fix: in `_unit_state_to_json` map patrol_route to [[x,y],...] and in `_unit_state_from_json` rebuild Vector2i + int(patrol_index); add a test that autosaves a battle containing a patrolling enemy, resumes it via `_try_resume_battle_progress`, and asserts `patrol_route[0] is Vector2i` and one `_patrol_step` runs without error. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] LIVE BUG: the mid-battle autosave is one global slot, not per save slot — battle.gd:2834 `_BATTLE_PROGRESS_SAVE_NAME := "battle_in_progress"` is used for save/load/delete (2918/2923/2930) with no `RosterManager.current_slot` prefix, and resume only matches on `mission_path` (2931). Quit mid-mission in Slot 1, start the same mission in Slot 2, and you resume Slot 1's soldiers/credits-in-progress, then `sync_from_battle` writes them into Slot 2's roster. Fix: add `_battle_progress_save_name()` returning `"%s_battle_in_progress" % slot` (plain name when current_slot is empty) and use it in all three call sites; also delete it in save_slot_select.gd `_on_slot_deleted` (line ~200, which only removes the slot's own suffixed saves). Add tests in tests/test_battle_persistence.gd: autosave under slot1, `RosterManager.load_slot("slot2")`, assert `_try_resume_battle_progress()` returns false. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Make SaveManager writes atomic with a one-deep backup — scripts/save/save_manager.gd:66-72 opens the final path with FileAccess.WRITE (truncates immediately) then `store_string`; a crash/kill/power loss mid-write leaves a truncated file, which `load_save` (27-30) treats as "no save" so `RosterManager._ready` (roster_manager.gd:136-139) reseeds defaults and overwrites the campaign. Fix: write to `<name>.json.tmp`, close, then `DirAccess.rename_absolute` over the real file after first copying the old file to `<name>.json.bak`; in `load_save`, if the main file fails to parse/validate, fall back to `.bak`. Also reject `save_name` containing `/`, `\` or `..` (only blank names are rejected today, line 52). Extend tests/test_save_manager*.gd: truncated main file recovers from .bak, traversal name returns false, leftover .tmp is ignored by `list_saves` (only `.json` suffix counts, verify `.json.bak` is not listed). {cat: game; size: M; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Stop silently reseeding over a corrupt roster/tech save — roster_manager.gd `_load_roster` (541-569) and tech_manager.gd `_load_techs` (~176) return early on `{}` (missing OR corrupt, indistinguishable), after which `_ready` calls `_seed_default_roster()`/`_seed_default_techs()` which immediately `_save_roster()`/`_save_techs()` over the broken file, destroying any chance of manual recovery. Fix: add `SaveManager.file_exists_for(save_name)`; when the file exists but load returned {}, copy it to `<name>.corrupt-<unix_ts>.json` (new `SaveManager.quarantine(save_name)`) before seeding, and printerr which slot was quarantined. Test: write garbage to user://saves/roster.json, call `RosterManager.load_slot("")`, assert a `.corrupt-*` copy exists and roster was reseeded. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] TechManager never picks up balance changes or new techs for existing saves — `_save_techs` (tech_manager.gd:~162) persists each tech's `cost`, `description`, `branch`, `requires`, and `_load_techs` (~176-196) rebuilds `_techs` purely from the save, so editing a cost/description in `_seed_default_techs` (144-160) or adding an 8th tech never reaches a player who already has a "tech" save (and `apply_tech_bonuses` keys on tech names, so an old save missing e.g. "Combat Stims" can never research it). Fix: in `_load_techs`, build the list from the current default definitions and overlay only `researched` flags by `tech_name` from the save (ignore unknown saved names, drop stale cost/description). Refactor defaults into `_default_techs() -> Array[TechItem]` used by both paths. Tests in tests/test_tech_manager*.gd: a legacy save with old cost/missing tech loads with current costs and the new tech unresearched, researched flags preserved. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Harden mid-battle resume against incomplete/older payloads — battle.gd `_try_resume_battle_progress` (2929-2984) indexes `saved["obstacles"]`, `saved["cover_durability"]`, `["rubble_cells"]`, `["hazard_cells"]`, `["loot_cells"]`, `["units"]`, `["prisoners"]`, `["current_turn"]`, `["enemies_killed"]`, `["captured_enemies"]`, `["materials_collected"]` with no `.get` fallback, and `_unit_state_from_json` (2867) indexes `out["cell"]`/`out["modulate"]`; any missing key (older save format, partial write) is a hard script error when the player enters that mission. Fix: add a pure validator `BattleResumeValidator.is_valid(saved: Dictionary) -> bool` (new scripts/battle/battle_resume_validator.gd) that checks required keys + types, call it at the top of `_try_resume_battle_progress` (on failure: `_clear_battle_progress()`, printerr, return false so the normal `_spawn_units()` runs). Tests: validator unit tests for each missing key + a battle test feeding a truncated dict. {cat: game; size: M; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Result banner shows gross credits while upkeep is silently deducted, and reward math is duplicated — battle.gd `_end_battle` computes `credits_earned` inline (4445-4447) and then calls `RosterManager.pay_squad_upkeep(...)` (4458) whose return value is discarded, so the banner (`_show_result_banner`, `+%d credits`) overstates the gain by up to 40 credits per mission. Also scripts/battle/reward_calc.gd `RewardCalc.mission_payout(won, kills, captures, mission_reward, kill_reward, capture_reward)` is fully tested but unreferenced (no callers in scripts/). Fix: compute `credits_earned` via `RewardCalc.mission_payout(victory, _enemies_killed, _captured_enemies.size() if victory else 0, MISSION_REWARD, KILL_REWARD, CAPTURE_REWARD)` (same numbers), capture `var upkeep := RosterManager.pay_squad_upkeep(...)`, and pass `upkeep` into `_show_result_banner` to render an extra "-N upkeep" line when > 0. Add a test asserting payout equality with the old formula and the upkeep line text. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Bleed-out deaths skip the morale hit that every other soldier death applies — `_kill_or_down` (battle.gd:3930-3934) calls `_apply_morale_loss(u.team)` before `_remove_unit`, but `_tick_bleed_out_all` (3943-3947) does `u._become_corpse(); _remove_unit(u)` directly, so a soldier who bleeds out (a common death path, since downing is the default) costs his squad no morale. Extract a `_finalize_death(u)` helper (corpse + morale + remove) used by both sites, or call `_apply_morale_loss` in `_tick_bleed_out_all`. Test in tests/: two player units, one downed with bleed_out_turns=1, advance one round via `_tick_bleed_out_all()`, assert the survivor's `morale` dropped by MORALE_LOSS_PER_ALLY_DEATH. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Remove dead `_shot_aim` and the obsolete aim-space suppression dock — battle.gd:2319 `_shot_aim()` + const `SUPPRESS_AIM_PENALTY` (line 46) implement the OLD aim-space suppression that the comment at 2514 says was replaced by hit-space `Suppression.net_hit_percent`; grep shows `_shot_aim` has no callers in scripts/ (only tests/test_status_effects_battle.gd:79-94 exercise it), so two contradictory suppression models exist and a future caller would double-dock. Delete `_shot_aim` and the constant, rewrite those three tests to assert via `_calc_hit_chance(..., suppress_stacks=_suppress_stacks(u))` that a suppressed shooter's hit% is lower by up to SUPPRESS_HIT_CAP and never below the 10% floor. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Guard the repo's Godot .uid hygiene with a test and clean stragglers — `git ls-files` shows orphan `scripts/battle/cover_object.gd.uid` (the script lives in scripts/grid/) and `scripts/units/xp_curve.gd.uid` (script retired by the dedupe item), while six committed scripts have no `.uid` (scripts/mission/mission_directive.gd, mission_summary.gd, scripts/roster/equipment.gd, loadout_preset.gd, scar.gd, scripts/units/unit_faction.gd), which makes uid references unstable across machines once the editor regenerates them. Delete the two orphans and add tests/test_uid_hygiene.gd that walks res://scripts and res://tests with DirAccess asserting every `X.gd.uid` has a sibling `X.gd` (missing-uid direction: assert_true only for orphans; list missing ones via `gut.p` without failing, since Godot creates them on editor open). {cat: test; size: S; multifile: yes; research: repo}

## Manual research refuel 2026-10-05 (auto-research blocked: Claude usage limit until Oct 7) - 5 items, verified: save/load coercion gaps in roster+tech loaders, autosave validator test coverage, divergent dead XP curve
- [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Harden the roster save loader against corrupt scalar values — `scripts/roster/roster_manager.gd:562-566` `_load_roster()` assigns `_missions_completed`, `_credits` and `_materials` straight from `save_data.get(...)` with no `int()` coercion or floor, so a save holding a String (or a negative number) makes the later `+=` / `<` in `add_credits`, `spend_credits` and `spend_materials` (lines 200-275) error or leave the player with a negative balance; the project's own rule (AGENTS.md "Coerce types from loaded/serialized data") is not applied here, and JSON also round-trips whole numbers as floats. - [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Add charges field to Ability to close the API gap with AbilityStatsValidator and AbilityResolver — `scripts/units/ability_stats_validator.gd:12` validates `charges>=0` in ability dicts; `scripts/units/ability_resolver.gd:5,14` takes `charges: int`; but `scripts/units/ability.gd` has no `charges` field at all. - [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Fix Damage.expected_damage floors armor-mitigated damage at 1 but mitigated() and resolve_mitigated() floor at 0 — `scripts/battle/damage.gd:37` uses `maxi(resolved - armor, 1)` meaning the prediction function reports ≥1 expected damage even when armor would absorb all damage; `damage.gd:42` (`mitigated()`) and `:56` (`resolve_mitigated()`) use floor 0. When battle.gd wires both for the same shot, the telegraph display would show non-zero expected damage while the actual hit deals 0 — misleading the player. This creates false test coverage: the class looks exercised but the live damage path ignores it entirely. - [ ] [P1] [decomposed] Silent DOT damage scaling bug — `scripts/battle/battle.gd:3356-3358` calls `DotDamage.tick_damage(BURN_DOT, u.status.turns_left("burning"))`, passing turns-remaining as the `stacks` parameter. `DotDamage.tick_damage` (scripts/units/dot_damage.gd:4, verified) computes `base * stacks`, so a 3-turn burn deals 1×3+1×2+1×1=6 total HP instead of the documented flat 1+1+1=3 (BURN_DOT=1 per stack per turn, confirmed battle.gd:24 comment). The same bug applies to poison (battle.gd:3358). Fix: replace `u.status.turns_left("burning")` with `1` at both call sites — StatusEffects tracks only one application, so stacks is always 1. No new helpers needed; DotDamage already has the right function signature. {cat: game; size: S; multifile: no; research: none}

Add `tests/test_mission_validator_terrain.gd` with in-memory MissionData cases (valid passes; obstacle at (grid.x, 0) fails; door cell at (-1, 2) fails; high-ground on an obstacle fails) and one test that loads every `res://missions/mission_*.tres` via DirAccess and asserts `terrain_cells_ok` is true (a repo scan found all 59 mission files already satisfy it; list failing file names in the assert message). {cat: game; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P2] [decomposed] Mission validator: no duplicate or overlapping spawn cells — `scripts/mission/mission_validator.gd` has no check that two units are not placed on the same cell: duplicate entries inside `player_spawns` or `enemy_spawns`, or a cell present in both lists, are accepted by `MissionData.is_valid()` and then two units are placed on one tile at battle start. Add pure static `MissionValidator.spawns_unique_ok(m: MissionData) -> bool` returning false if any cell appears twice across `player_spawns` + `enemy_spawns` combined, and call it from `MissionData.is_valid()` in `scripts/mission/mission_data.gd`. Add `tests/test_mission_validator_unique_spawns.gd`: in-memory valid mission passes; a repeated player spawn fails; the same cell in both player and enemy lists fails; plus a test loading every `res://missions/mission_*.tres` via DirAccess asserting `spawns_unique_ok` is true (a repo scan found no duplicates in any of the 59 mission files; list failures by file name). {cat: game; size: S; multifile: yes; research: none}
- [ ] [P3] [decomposed] Mission validator: objective cells must be reachable and parameters sane — `scripts/mission/mission_validator.gd` never checks that the cell a mission is won on is not solid, nor that objective parameters make sense: an `extraction_zone` or `target_cell` sitting on an obstacle makes the mission unwinnable; `objective_type` outside 0-5, a SURVIVE mission (type 2) with `max_turns < 1`, or `extraction_radius < 0` are silently accepted. Call it from `MissionData.is_valid()` in `scripts/mission/mission_data.gd`. Add `tests/test_mission_validator_objective.gd` with in-memory cases for each rule plus a test loading every `res://missions/mission_*.tres` via DirAccess asserting `objective_ok` is true (a repo scan of mission_*.tres found none violating these rules; list failures by file name). Do not touch battle.gd. {cat: game; size: M; multifile: yes; research: none}

Extract the string into pure static `MissionHub.status_text(slot_name: String, completed: int, total: int, credits: int, materials: int) -> String` that returns `"Slot: %s      Missions cleared: %d / %d      Credits: %d      Materials: %d"` using "—" for an empty slot name, `mini(maxi(completed, 0), maxi(total, 0))` for the cleared count and `maxi(credits, 0)`/`maxi(materials, 0)`, and call it from `_ready()` with `RosterManager.get_materials()`.
